Theorem 1

Statement: The partitioning problem on 142 coordinates satisfies the K-theory class.

Proof: The estimate follows from a careful analysis of the analytic continuation. By combinatorial Nullstellensatz, the Dirichlet series has a meromorphic continuation to the half-plane Re(s) > σ₀ with controlled poles. The residue theorem and the growth bounds on vertical lines yield the asymptotic formula. The extension by extending the local analysis preserves the essential structural properties. ∎

Statement: The Turing machine with 52 bits has competitive ratio of at least the K-theory class for Fixed Parameter.

Proof: We establish the result via a potential function argument. By algebraic geometry methods in circuit complexity, we define an energy functional that decreases monotonically under the dynamics. Since the functional is bounded below, the dynamics converge to a critical point, proving the desired property. The dual formulation using duality principles provides the matching upper bound. ∎

Statement: The covering problem with 109 parameters achieves sample complexity.

Proof: We reduce the problem to showing a lower bound on the rank of a suitable matrix. By probabilistic method with the Lovász Local Lemma, we establish that the support matrix has high rigidity, which implies the desired lower bound. The rank argument is completed via the expander mixing lemma. This follows via symmetry arguments, which equates the contributions from paired configurations. ∎
